^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  183) # math.sumprod is available for Python 3.12+
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  184) try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  185)     from math import sumprod as _fsumprod
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  186) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  187) except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  188)     # Extended precision algorithms from T. J. Dekker,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  189)     # "A Floating-Point Technique for Extending the Available Precision"
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  190)     # https://csclub.uwaterloo.ca/~pbarfuss/dekker1971.pdf
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  191)     # Formulas: (5.5) (5.6) and (5.8).  Code: mul12()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  192) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  193)     def dl_split(x: float):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  194)         "Split a float into two half-precision components."
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  195)         t = x * 134217729.0  # Veltkamp constant = 2.0 ** 27 + 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  196)         hi = t - (t - x)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  197)         lo = x - hi
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  198)         return hi, lo
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  199) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  200)     def dl_mul(x, y):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  201)         "Lossless multiplication."
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  202)         xx_hi, xx_lo = dl_split(x)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  203)         yy_hi, yy_lo = dl_split(y)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  204)         p = xx_hi * yy_hi
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  205)         q = xx_hi * yy_lo + xx_lo * yy_hi
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  206)         z = p + q
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  207)         zz = p - z + q + xx_lo * yy_lo
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  208)         return z, zz
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  209) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  210)     def _fsumprod(p, q):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  211)         return fsum(chain.from_iterable(map(dl_mul, p, q)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  212) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  213) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  214) def chunked(iterable, n, strict=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  215)     """Break *iterable* into lists of length *n*:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  216) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  217)         >>> list(chunked([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6], 3))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  218)         [[1, 2, 3], [4, 5, 6]]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  219) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  220)     By the default, the last yielded list will have fewer than *n* elements
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  221)     if the length of *iterable* is not divisible by *n*:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  222) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  223)         >>> list(chunked([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8], 3))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  224)         [[1, 2, 3], [4, 5, 6], [7, 8]]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  225) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  226)     To use a fill-in value instead, see the :func:`grouper` recipe.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  227) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  228)     If the length of *iterable* is not divisible by *n* and *strict* is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  229)     ``True``, then ``ValueError`` will be raised before the last
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  230)     list is yielded.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  231) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  232)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  233)     if n is not None and n < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  234)         raise ValueError('n must be at least 0')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  235) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  236)     iterator = iter(partial(take, n, iter(iterable)), [])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  237)     if strict:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  238)         if n is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  239)             raise ValueError('n must not be None when using strict mode.')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  240) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  241)         def ret():
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  242)             for chunk in iterator:

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  245)                 yield chunk
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  246) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  247)         return ret()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  248)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  249)         return iterator
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  250) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  251)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  277) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  278)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  302) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  303)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  319) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  320) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  321) class peekable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  322)     """Wrap an iterator to allow lookahead and prepending elements.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  323) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  324)     Call :meth:`peek` on the result to get the value that will be returned
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  325)     by :func:`next`. This won't advance the iterator:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  326) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  327)         >>> p = peekable(['a', 'b'])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  328)         >>> p.peek()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  329)         'a'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  330)         >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  331)         'a'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  332) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  333)     Pass :meth:`peek` a default value to return that instead of raising
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  334)     ``StopIteration`` when the iterator is exhausted.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  335) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  336)         >>> p = peekable([])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  337)         >>> p.peek('hi')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  338)         'hi'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  339) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  340)     peekables also offer a :meth:`prepend` method, which "inserts" items
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  341)     at the head of the iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  342) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  343)         >>> p = peekable([1, 2, 3])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  344)         >>> p.prepend(10, 11, 12)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  345)         >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  346)         10
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  347)         >>> p.peek()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  348)         11
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  349)         >>> list(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  350)         [11, 12, 1, 2, 3]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  351) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  352)     peekables can be indexed. Index 0 is the item that will be returned by
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  353)     :func:`next`, index 1 is the item after that, and so on:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  354)     The values up to the given index will be cached.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  355) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  356)         >>> p = peekable(['a', 'b', 'c', 'd'])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  357)         >>> p[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  358)         'a'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  359)         >>> p[1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  360)         'b'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  361)         >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  362)         'a'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  363) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  364)     Negative indexes are supported, but be aware that they will cache the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  365)     remaining items in the source iterator, which may require significant
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  366)     storage.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  367) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  368)     To check whether a peekable is exhausted, check its truth value:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  369) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  370)         >>> p = peekable(['a', 'b'])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  371)         >>> if p:  # peekable has items
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  372)         ...     list(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  373)         ['a', 'b']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  374)         >>> if not p:  # peekable is exhausted
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  375)         ...     list(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  376)         []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  377) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  378)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  379) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  380)     def __init__(self, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  381)         self._it = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  382)         self._cache = deque()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  383) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  384)     def __iter__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  385)         return self
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  386) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  387)     def __bool__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  388)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  389)             self.peek()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  390)         except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  391)             return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  392)         return True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  393) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  394)     def peek(self, default=_marker):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  395)         """Return the item that will be next returned from ``next()``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  396) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  397)         Return ``default`` if there are no items left. If ``default`` is not
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  398)         provided, raise ``StopIteration``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  399) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  400)         """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  401)         if not self._cache:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  402)             try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  403)                 self._cache.append(next(self._it))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  404)             except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  405)                 if default is _marker:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  406)                     raise
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  407)                 return default
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  408)         return self._cache[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  409) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  410)     def prepend(self, *items):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  411)         """Stack up items to be the next ones returned from ``next()`` or
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  412)         ``self.peek()``. The items will be returned in
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  413)         first in, first out order::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  414) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  415)             >>> p = peekable([1, 2, 3])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  416)             >>> p.prepend(10, 11, 12)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  417)             >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  418)             10
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  419)             >>> list(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  420)             [11, 12, 1, 2, 3]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  421) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  422)         It is possible, by prepending items, to "resurrect" a peekable that
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  423)         previously raised ``StopIteration``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  424) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  425)             >>> p = peekable([])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  426)             >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  427)             Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  428)               ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  429)             StopIteration
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  430)             >>> p.prepend(1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  431)             >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  432)             1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  433)             >>> next(p)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  434)             Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  435)               ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  436)             StopIteration
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  437) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  438)         """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  439)         self._cache.extendleft(reversed(items))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  440) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  441)     __class_getitem__ = classmethod(types.GenericAlias)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  442) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  443)     def __next__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  444)         if self._cache:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  445)             return self._cache.popleft()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  446) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  447)         return next(self._it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  448) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  449)     def _get_slice(self, index):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  450)         # Normalize the slice's arguments
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  451)         step = 1 if (index.step is None) else index.step
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  452)         if step > 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  453)             start = 0 if (index.start is None) else index.start
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  454)             stop = maxsize if (index.stop is None) else index.stop
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  455)         elif step < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  456)             start = -1 if (index.start is None) else index.start
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  457)             stop = (-maxsize - 1) if (index.stop is None) else index.stop
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  458)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  459)             raise ValueError('slice step cannot be zero')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  460) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  461)         # If either the start or stop index is negative, we'll need to cache
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  462)         # the rest of the iterable in order to slice from the right side.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  463)         if (start < 0) or (stop < 0):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  464)             self._cache.extend(self._it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  465)         # Otherwise we'll need to find the rightmost index and cache to that
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  466)         # point.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  467)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  468)             n = min(max(start, stop) + 1, maxsize)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  469)             cache_len = len(self._cache)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  470)             if n >= cache_len:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  471)                 self._cache.extend(islice(self._it, n - cache_len))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  472) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  473)         return list(self._cache)[index]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  474) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  475)     def __getitem__(self, index):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  476)         if isinstance(index, slice):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  477)             return self._get_slice(index)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  478) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  479)         cache_len = len(self._cache)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  480)         if index < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  481)             self._cache.extend(self._it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  482)         elif index >= cache_len:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  483)             self._cache.extend(islice(self._it, index + 1 - cache_len))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  484) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  485)         return self._cache[index]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  486) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  487) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  488) def consumer(func):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  489)     """Decorator that automatically advances a PEP-342-style "reverse iterator"
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  490)     to its first yield point so you don't have to call ``next()`` on it
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  491)     manually.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  492) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  493)         >>> @consumer
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  494)         ... def tally():
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  495)         ...     i = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  496)         ...     while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  497)         ...         print('Thing number %s is %s.' % (i, (yield)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  498)         ...         i += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  499)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  500)         >>> t = tally()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  501)         >>> t.send('red')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  502)         Thing number 0 is red.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  503)         >>> t.send('fish')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  504)         Thing number 1 is fish.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  505) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  506)     Without the decorator, you would have to call ``next(t)`` before
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  507)     ``t.send()`` could be used.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  508) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  509)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  510) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  511)     @wraps(func)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  512)     def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  513)         gen = func(*args, **kwargs)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  514)         next(gen)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  515)         return gen
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  516) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  517)     return wrapper
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  518) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  519) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  520) def ilen(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  521)     """Return the number of items in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  522) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  523)     For example, there are 168 prime numbers below 1,000:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  524) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  525)         >>> ilen(sieve(1000))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  526)         168
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  527) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  528)     Equivalent to, but faster than::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  529) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  530)         def ilen(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  531)             count = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  532)             for _ in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  533)                 count += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  534)             return count
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  535) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  536)     This fully consumes the iterable, so handle with care.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  537) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  538)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  539)     # This is the "most beautiful of the fast variants" of this function.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  540)     # If you think you can improve on it, please ensure that your version
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  541)     # is both 10x faster and 10x more beautiful.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  542)     return sum(compress(repeat(1), zip(iterable)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  543) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  544) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  545) def iterate(func, start):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  546)     """Return ``start``, ``func(start)``, ``func(func(start))``, ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  547) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  548)     Produces an infinite iterator. To add a stopping condition,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  549)     use :func:`take`, ``takewhile``, or :func:`takewhile_inclusive`:.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  550) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  551)     >>> take(10, iterate(lambda x: 2*x, 1))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  552)     [1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  553) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  554)     >>> collatz = lambda x: 3*x + 1 if x%2==1 else x // 2
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  555)     >>> list(takewhile_inclusive(lambda x: x!=1, iterate(collatz, 10)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  556)     [10, 5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  557) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  558)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  559)     with suppress(StopIteration):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  560)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  561)             yield start
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  562)             start = func(start)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  563) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  564) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  565) def with_iter(context_manager):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  566)     """Wrap an iterable in a ``with`` statement, so it closes once exhausted.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  567) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  568)     For example, this will close the file when the iterator is exhausted::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  569) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  570)         upper_lines = (line.upper() for line in with_iter(open('foo')))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  571) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  572)     Note that you have to actually exhaust the iterator for opened files to be closed.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  573) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  574)     Any context manager which returns an iterable is a candidate for
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  575)     ``with_iter``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  576) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  577)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  578)     with context_manager as iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  579)         yield from iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  580) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  581) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  582) class sized_iterator:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  583)     """Wrapper for *iterable* that implements ``__len__``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  584) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  585)     >>> it = map(str, range(5))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  586)     >>> sized_it = sized_iterator(it, 5)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  587)     >>> len(sized_it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  588)     5
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  589)     >>> list(sized_it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  590)     ['0', '1', '2', '3', '4']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  591) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  592)     This is useful for tools that use :func:`len`, like
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  593)     `tqdm <https://pypi.org/project/tqdm/>`__ .
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  594) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  595)     The wrapper doesn't validate the provided *length*, so be sure to choose
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  596)     a value that reflects reality.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  597)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  598) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  599)     def __init__(self, iterable, length):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  600)         self._iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  601)         self._length = length
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  602) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  603)     def __next__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  604)         return next(self._iterator)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  605) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  606)     def __iter__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  607)         return self
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  608) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  609)     def __len__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  610)         return self._length
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  611) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  612) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  613) def one(iterable, too_short=None, too_long=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  614)     """Return the first item from *iterable*, which is expected to contain only
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  615)     that item. Raise an exception if *iterable* is empty or has more than one
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  616)     item.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  617) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  618)     :func:`one` is useful for ensuring that an iterable contains only one item.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  619)     For example, it can be used to retrieve the result of a database query
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  620)     that is expected to return a single row.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  621) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  622)     If *iterable* is empty, ``ValueError`` will be raised. You may specify a
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  623)     different exception with the *too_short* keyword:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  624) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  625)         >>> it = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  626)         >>> one(it)  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  627)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  628)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  629)         ValueError: too few items in iterable (expected 1)'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  630)         >>> too_short = IndexError('too few items')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  631)         >>> one(it, too_short=too_short)  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  632)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  633)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  634)         IndexError: too few items
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  635) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  636)     Similarly, if *iterable* contains more than one item, ``ValueError`` will
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  637)     be raised. You may specify a different exception with the *too_long*
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  638)     keyword:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  639) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  640)         >>> it = ['too', 'many']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  641)         >>> one(it)  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  642)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  643)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  644)         ValueError: Expected exactly one item in iterable, but got 'too',
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  645)         'many', and perhaps more.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  646)         >>> too_long = RuntimeError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  647)         >>> one(it, too_long=too_long)  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  648)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  649)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  650)         RuntimeError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  651) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  652)     Note that :func:`one` attempts to advance *iterable* twice to ensure there
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  653)     is only one item. See :func:`spy` or :func:`peekable` to check iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  654)     contents less destructively.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  655) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  656)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  657)     iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  658)     for first in iterator:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  659)         for second in iterator:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  660)             msg = (
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  661)                 f'Expected exactly one item in iterable, but got {first!r}, '
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  662)                 f'{second!r}, and perhaps more.'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  663)             )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  664)             raise too_long or ValueError(msg)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  665)         return first
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  666)     raise too_short or ValueError('too few items in iterable (expected 1)')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  667) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  668) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  669) def raise_(exception, *args):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  670)     raise exception(*args)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  671) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  672) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  673) def strictly_n(iterable, n, too_short=None, too_long=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  674)     """Validate that *iterable* has exactly *n* items and return them if
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  675)     it does. If it has fewer than *n* items, call function *too_short*
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  676)     with the actual number of items. If it has more than *n* items, call function
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  677)     *too_long* with the number ``n + 1``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  678) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  679)         >>> iterable = ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  680)         >>> n = 4
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  681)         >>> list(strictly_n(iterable, n))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  682)         ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  683) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  684)     Note that the returned iterable must be consumed in order for the check to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  685)     be made.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  686) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  687)     By default, *too_short* and *too_long* are functions that raise
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  688)     ``ValueError``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  689) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  690)         >>> list(strictly_n('ab', 3))  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  691)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  692)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  693)         ValueError: too few items in iterable (got 2)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  694) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  695)         >>> list(strictly_n('abc', 2))  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  696)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  697)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  698)         ValueError: too many items in iterable (got at least 3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  699) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  700)     You can instead supply functions that do something else.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  701)     *too_short* will be called with the number of items in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  702)     *too_long* will be called with `n + 1`.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  703) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  704)         >>> def too_short(item_count):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  705)         ...     raise RuntimeError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  706)         >>> it = strictly_n('abcd', 6, too_short=too_short)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  707)         >>> list(it)  # doctest: +IGNORE_EXCEPTION_DETAIL
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  708)         Traceback (most recent call last):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  709)         ...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  710)         RuntimeError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  711) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  712)         >>> def too_long(item_count):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  713)         ...     print('The boss is going to hear about this')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  714)         >>> it = strictly_n('abcdef', 4, too_long=too_long)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  715)         >>> list(it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  716)         The boss is going to hear about this
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  717)         ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  718) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  719)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  720)     if too_short is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  721)         too_short = lambda item_count: raise_(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  722)             ValueError,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  723)             f'Too few items in iterable (got {item_count})',
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  724)         )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  725) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  726)     if too_long is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  727)         too_long = lambda item_count: raise_(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  728)             ValueError,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  729)             f'Too many items in iterable (got at least {item_count})',
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  730)         )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  731) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  732)     it = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  733) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  734)     sent = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  735)     for item in islice(it, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  736)         yield item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  737)         sent += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  738) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  739)     if sent < n:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  740)         too_short(sent)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  741)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  742) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  743)     for item in it:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  744)         too_long(n + 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  745)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  746) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  747) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  748) def distinct_permutations(iterable, r=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  749)     """Yield successive distinct permutations of the elements in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  750) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  751)         >>> sorted(distinct_permutations([1, 0, 1]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  752)         [(0, 1, 1), (1, 0, 1), (1, 1, 0)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  753) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  754)     Equivalent to yielding from ``set(permutations(iterable))``, except
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  755)     duplicates are not generated and thrown away. For larger input sequences
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  756)     this is much more efficient.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  757) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  758)     If the elements of the input iterable are sortable, the output tuples are
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  759)     produced in sorted order.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  760) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  761)     Duplicate permutations arise when there are duplicated elements in the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  762)     input iterable. The number of items returned is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  763)     `n! / (x_1! * x_2! * ... * x_n!)`, where `n` is the total number of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  764)     items input, and each `x_i` is the count of a distinct item in the input
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  765)     sequence. The function :func:`multinomial` computes this directly.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  766) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  767)     If *r* is given, only the *r*-length permutations are yielded.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  768) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  769)         >>> sorted(distinct_permutations([1, 0, 1], r=2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  770)         [(0, 1), (1, 0), (1, 1)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  771)         >>> sorted(distinct_permutations(range(3), r=2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  772)         [(0, 1), (0, 2), (1, 0), (1, 2), (2, 0), (2, 1)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  773) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  774)     *iterable* need not be sortable, but note that using equal (``x == y``)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  775)     but non-identical (``id(x) != id(y)``) elements may produce surprising
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  776)     behavior. For example, ``1`` and ``True`` are equal but non-identical:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  777) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  778)         >>> list(distinct_permutations([1, True, '3']))  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  779)         [
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  780)             (1, True, '3'),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  781)             (1, '3', True),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  782)             ('3', 1, True)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  783)         ]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  784)         >>> list(distinct_permutations([1, 2, '3']))  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  785)         [
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  786)             (1, 2, '3'),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  787)             (1, '3', 2),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  788)             (2, 1, '3'),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  789)             (2, '3', 1),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  790)             ('3', 1, 2),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  791)             ('3', 2, 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  792)         ]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  793)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  794) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  795)     # Algorithm: https://w.wiki/Qai
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  796)     def _full(A):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  797)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  798)             # Yield the permutation we have
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  799)             yield tuple(A)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  800) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  801)             # Find the largest index i such that A[i] < A[i + 1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  802)             for i in range(size - 2, -1, -1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  803)                 if A[i] < A[i + 1]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  804)                     break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  805)             #  If no such index exists, this permutation is the last one
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  806)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  807)                 return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  808) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  809)             # Find the largest index j greater than j such that A[i] < A[j]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  810)             for j in range(size - 1, i, -1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  811)                 if A[i] < A[j]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  812)                     break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  813) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  814)             # Swap the value of A[i] with that of A[j], then reverse the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  815)             # sequence from A[i + 1] to form the new permutation
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  816)             A[i], A[j] = A[j], A[i]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  817)             A[i + 1 :] = A[: i - size : -1]  # A[i + 1:][::-1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  818) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  819)     # Algorithm: modified from the above
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  820)     def _partial(A, r):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  821)         # Split A into the first r items and the last r items
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  822)         head, tail = A[:r], A[r:]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  823)         right_head_indexes = range(r - 1, -1, -1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  824)         left_tail_indexes = range(len(tail))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  825) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  826)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  827)             # Yield the permutation we have
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  828)             yield tuple(head)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  829) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  830)             # Starting from the right, find the first index of the head with
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  831)             # value smaller than the maximum value of the tail - call it i.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  832)             pivot = tail[-1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  833)             for i in right_head_indexes:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  834)                 if head[i] < pivot:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  835)                     break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  836)                 pivot = head[i]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  837)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  838)                 return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  839) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  840)             # Starting from the left, find the first value of the tail
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  841)             # with a value greater than head[i] and swap.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  842)             for j in left_tail_indexes:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  843)                 if tail[j] > head[i]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  844)                     head[i], tail[j] = tail[j], head[i]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  845)                     break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  846)             # If we didn't find one, start from the right and find the first
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  847)             # index of the head with a value greater than head[i] and swap.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  848)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  849)                 for j in right_head_indexes:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  850)                     if head[j] > head[i]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  851)                         head[i], head[j] = head[j], head[i]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  852)                         break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  853) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  854)             # Reverse head[i + 1:] and swap it with tail[:r - (i + 1)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  855)             tail += head[: i - r : -1]  # head[i + 1:][::-1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  856)             i += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  857)             head[i:], tail[:] = tail[: r - i], tail[r - i :]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  858) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  859)     items = list(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  860) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  861)     try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  862)         items.sort()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  863)         sortable = True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  864)     except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  865)         sortable = False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  866) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  867)         indices_dict = defaultdict(list)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  868) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  869)         for item in items:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  870)             indices_dict[items.index(item)].append(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  871) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  872)         indices = [items.index(item) for item in items]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  873)         indices.sort()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  874) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  875)         equivalent_items = {k: cycle(v) for k, v in indices_dict.items()}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  876) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  877)         def permuted_items(permuted_indices):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  878)             return tuple(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  879)                 next(equivalent_items[index]) for index in permuted_indices
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  880)             )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  881) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  882)     size = len(items)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  883)     if r is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  884)         r = size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  885) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  886)     # functools.partial(_partial, ... )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  887)     algorithm = _full if (r == size) else partial(_partial, r=r)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  888) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  889)     if 0 < r <= size:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  890)         if sortable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  891)             return algorithm(items)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  892)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  893)             return (
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  894)                 permuted_items(permuted_indices)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  895)                 for permuted_indices in algorithm(indices)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  896)             )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  897) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  898)     return iter(() if r else ((),))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  899) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  900) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  901) def derangements(iterable, r=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  902)     """Yield successive derangements of the elements in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  903) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  904)     A derangement is a permutation in which no element appears at its original
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  905)     index. In other words, a derangement is a permutation that has no fixed points.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  906) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  907)     Suppose Alice, Bob, Carol, and Dave are playing Secret Santa.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  908)     The code below outputs all of the different ways to assign gift recipients
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  909)     such that nobody is assigned to himself or herself:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  910) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  911)         >>> for d in derangements(['Alice', 'Bob', 'Carol', 'Dave']):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  912)         ...    print(', '.join(d))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  913)         Bob, Alice, Dave, Carol
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  914)         Bob, Carol, Dave, Alice
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  915)         Bob, Dave, Alice, Carol
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  916)         Carol, Alice, Dave, Bob
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  917)         Carol, Dave, Alice, Bob
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  918)         Carol, Dave, Bob, Alice
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  919)         Dave, Alice, Bob, Carol
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  920)         Dave, Carol, Alice, Bob
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  921)         Dave, Carol, Bob, Alice
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  922) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  923)     If *r* is given, only the *r*-length derangements are yielded.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  924) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  925)         >>> sorted(derangements(range(3), 2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  926)         [(1, 0), (1, 2), (2, 0)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  927)         >>> sorted(derangements([0, 2, 3], 2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  928)         [(2, 0), (2, 3), (3, 0)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  929) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  930)     Elements are treated as unique based on their position, not on their value.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  931) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  932)     Consider the Secret Santa example with two *different* people who have
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  933)     the *same* name. Then there are two valid gift assignments even though
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  934)     it might appear that a person is assigned to themselves:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  935) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  936)         >>> names = ['Alice', 'Bob', 'Bob']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  937)         >>> list(derangements(names))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  938)         [('Bob', 'Bob', 'Alice'), ('Bob', 'Alice', 'Bob')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  939) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  940)     To avoid confusion, make the inputs distinct:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  941) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  942)         >>> deduped = [f'{name}{index}' for index, name in enumerate(names)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  943)         >>> list(derangements(deduped))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  944)         [('Bob1', 'Bob2', 'Alice0'), ('Bob2', 'Alice0', 'Bob1')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  945) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  946)     The number of derangements of a set of size *n* is known as the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  947)     "subfactorial of n".  For n > 0, the subfactorial is:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  948)     ``round(math.factorial(n) / math.e)``.   The more-itertools function
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  949)     :func:`subfactorial` computes this directly.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  950) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  951)     References:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  952) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  953)     * Article:  https://www.numberanalytics.com/blog/ultimate-guide-to-derangements-in-combinatorics
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  954)     * Sizes:    https://oeis.org/A000166
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  955)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  956)     xs = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  957)     ys = tuple(range(len(xs)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  958)     return compress(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  959)         permutations(xs, r=r),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  960)         map(all, map(map, repeat(is_not), repeat(ys), permutations(ys, r=r))),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  961)     )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  962) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  963) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  964) def intersperse(e, iterable, n=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  965)     """Intersperse filler element *e* among the items in *iterable*, leaving
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  966)     *n* items between each filler element.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  967) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  968)         >>> list(intersperse('!', [1, 2, 3, 4, 5]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  969)         [1, '!', 2, '!', 3, '!', 4, '!', 5]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  970) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  971)         >>> list(intersperse(None, [1, 2, 3, 4, 5], n=2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  972)         [1, 2, None, 3, 4, None, 5]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  973) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  974)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  975)     if n == 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  976)         raise ValueError('n must be > 0')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  977)     elif n == 1:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  978)         # interleave(repeat(e), iterable) -> e, x_0, e, x_1, e, x_2...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  979)         # islice(..., 1, None) -> x_0, e, x_1, e, x_2...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  980)         return islice(interleave(repeat(e), iterable), 1, None)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  981)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  982)         # interleave(filler, chunks) -> [e], [x_0, x_1], [e], [x_2, x_3]...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  983)         # islice(..., 1, None) -> [x_0, x_1], [e], [x_2, x_3]...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  984)         # flatten(...) -> x_0, x_1, e, x_2, x_3...
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  985)         filler = repeat([e])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  986)         chunks = chunked(iterable, n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  987)         return flatten(islice(interleave(filler, chunks), 1, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  988) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  989) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  990) def unique_to_each(*iterables):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  991)     """Return the elements from each of the input iterables that aren't in the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  992)     other input iterables.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  993) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  994)     For example, suppose you have a set of packages, each with a set of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  995)     dependencies::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  996) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  997)         {'pkg_1': {'A', 'B'}, 'pkg_2': {'B', 'C'}, 'pkg_3': {'B', 'D'}}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  998) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  999)     If you remove one package, which dependencies can also be removed?
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1000) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1001)     If ``pkg_1`` is removed, then ``A`` is no longer necessary - it is not
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1002)     associated with ``pkg_2`` or ``pkg_3``. Similarly, ``C`` is only needed for
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1003)     ``pkg_2``, and ``D`` is only needed for ``pkg_3``::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1004) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1005)         >>> unique_to_each({'A', 'B'}, {'B', 'C'}, {'B', 'D'})
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1006)         [['A'], ['C'], ['D']]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1007) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1008)     If there are duplicates in one input iterable that aren't in the others
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1009)     they will be duplicated in the output. Input order is preserved::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1010) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1011)         >>> unique_to_each("mississippi", "missouri")
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1012)         [['p', 'p'], ['o', 'u', 'r']]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1013) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1014)     It is assumed that the elements of each iterable are hashable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1015) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1016)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1017)     pool = [list(it) for it in iterables]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1018)     counts = Counter(chain.from_iterable(map(set, pool)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1019)     uniques = {element for element in counts if counts[element] == 1}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1020)     return [list(filter(uniques.__contains__, it)) for it in pool]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1021) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1022) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1023) def windowed(seq, n, fillvalue=None, step=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1024)     """Return a sliding window of width *n* over the given iterable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1025) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1026)         >>> all_windows = windowed([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], 3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1027)         >>> list(all_windows)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1028)         [(1, 2, 3), (2, 3, 4), (3, 4, 5)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1029) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1030)     When the window is larger than the iterable, *fillvalue* is used in place
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1031)     of missing values:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1032) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1033)         >>> list(windowed([1, 2, 3], 4))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1034)         [(1, 2, 3, None)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1035) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1036)     Each window will advance in increments of *step*:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1037) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1038)         >>> list(windowed([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6], 3, fillvalue='!', step=2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1039)         [(1, 2, 3), (3, 4, 5), (5, 6, '!')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1040) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1041)     To slide into the iterable's items, use :func:`chain` to add filler items
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1042)     to the left:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1043) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1044)         >>> iterable = [1, 2, 3, 4]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1045)         >>> n = 3
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1046)         >>> padding = [None] * (n - 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1047)         >>> list(windowed(chain(padding, iterable), 3))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1048)         [(None, None, 1), (None, 1, 2), (1, 2, 3), (2, 3, 4)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1049)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1050)     if n <= 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1051)         raise ValueError('n must be > 0')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1052)     if step < 1:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1053)         raise ValueError('step must be >= 1')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1054) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1055)     iterator = iter(seq)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1056) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1057)     # Generate first window
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1058)     window = deque(islice(iterator, n), maxlen=n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1059) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1060)     # Deal with the first window not being full
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1061)     if not window:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1062)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1063)     if len(window) < n:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1064)         yield tuple(window) + ((fillvalue,) * (n - len(window)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1065)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1066)     yield tuple(window)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1067) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1068)     # Create the filler for the next windows. The padding ensures
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1069)     # we have just enough elements to fill the last window.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1070)     padding = (fillvalue,) * (n - 1 if step >= n else step - 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1071)     filler = map(window.append, chain(iterator, padding))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1072) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1073)     # Generate the rest of the windows
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1074)     for _ in islice(filler, step - 1, None, step):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1075)         yield tuple(window)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1076) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1077) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1078) def substrings(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1079)     """Yield all of the substrings of *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1080) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1081)         >>> [''.join(s) for s in substrings('more')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1082)         ['m', 'o', 'r', 'e', 'mo', 'or', 're', 'mor', 'ore', 'more']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1083) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1084)     Note that non-string iterables can also be subdivided.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1085) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1086)         >>> list(substrings([0, 1, 2]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1087)         [(0,), (1,), (2,), (0, 1), (1, 2), (0, 1, 2)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1088) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1089)     Like subslices() but returns tuples instead of lists
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1090)     and returns the shortest substrings first.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1091) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1092)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1093)     seq = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1094)     item_count = len(seq)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1095)     for n in range(1, item_count + 1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1096)         slices = map(slice, range(item_count), range(n, item_count + 1))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1097)         yield from map(getitem, repeat(seq), slices)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1098) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1099) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1100) def substrings_indexes(seq, reverse=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1101)     """Yield all substrings and their positions in *seq*
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1102) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1103)     The items yielded will be a tuple of the form ``(substr, i, j)``, where
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1104)     ``substr == seq[i:j]``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1105) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1106)     This function only works for iterables that support slicing, such as
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1107)     ``str`` objects.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1108) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1109)     >>> for item in substrings_indexes('more'):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1110)     ...    print(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1111)     ('m', 0, 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1112)     ('o', 1, 2)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1113)     ('r', 2, 3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1114)     ('e', 3, 4)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1115)     ('mo', 0, 2)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1116)     ('or', 1, 3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1117)     ('re', 2, 4)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1118)     ('mor', 0, 3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1119)     ('ore', 1, 4)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1120)     ('more', 0, 4)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1121) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1122)     Set *reverse* to ``True`` to yield the same items in the opposite order.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1123) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1124) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1125)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1126)     r = range(1, len(seq) + 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1127)     if reverse:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1128)         r = reversed(r)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1129)     return (
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1130)         (seq[i : i + L], i, i + L) for L in r for i in range(len(seq) - L + 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1131)     )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1132) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1133) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1134) class bucket:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1135)     """Wrap *iterable* and return an object that buckets the iterable into
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1136)     child iterables based on a *key* function.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1137) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1138)         >>> iterable = ['a1', 'b1', 'c1', 'a2', 'b2', 'c2', 'b3']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1139)         >>> s = bucket(iterable, key=lambda x: x[0])  # Bucket by 1st character
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1140)         >>> sorted(list(s))  # Get the keys
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1141)         ['a', 'b', 'c']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1142)         >>> a_iterable = s['a']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1143)         >>> next(a_iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1144)         'a1'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1145)         >>> next(a_iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1146)         'a2'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1147)         >>> list(s['b'])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1148)         ['b1', 'b2', 'b3']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1149) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1150)     The original iterable will be advanced and its items will be cached until
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1151)     they are used by the child iterables. This may require significant storage.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1152) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1153)     By default, attempting to select a bucket to which no items belong  will
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1154)     exhaust the iterable and cache all values.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1155)     If you specify a *validator* function, selected buckets will instead be
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1156)     checked against it.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1157) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1158)         >>> from itertools import count
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1159)         >>> it = count(1, 2)  # Infinite sequence of odd numbers
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1160)         >>> key = lambda x: x % 10  # Bucket by last digit
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1161)         >>> validator = lambda x: x in {1, 3, 5, 7, 9}  # Odd digits only
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1162)         >>> s = bucket(it, key=key, validator=validator)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1163)         >>> 2 in s
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1164)         False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1165)         >>> list(s[2])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1166)         []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1167) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1168)     .. seealso:: :func:`map_reduce`, :func:`groupby_transform`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1169) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1170)     If storage is not a concern, :func:`map_reduce` returns a Python
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1171)     dictionary, which is generally easier to work with. If the elements
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1172)     with the same key are already adjacent, :func:`groupby_transform`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1173)     or :func:`itertools.groupby` can be used without any caching overhead.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1174) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1175)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1176) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1177)     def __init__(self, iterable, key, validator=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1178)         self._it = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1179)         self._key = key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1180)         self._cache = defaultdict(deque)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1181)         self._validator = validator or (lambda x: True)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1182) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1183)     def __contains__(self, value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1184)         if not self._validator(value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1185)             return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1186) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1187)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1188)             item = next(self[value])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1189)         except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1190)             return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1191)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1192)             self._cache[value].appendleft(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1193) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1194)         return True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1195) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1196)     def _get_values(self, value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1197)         """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1198)         Helper to yield items from the parent iterator that match *value*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1199)         Items that don't match are stored in the local cache as they
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1200)         are encountered.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1201)         """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1202)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1203)             # If we've cached some items that match the target value, emit
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1204)             # the first one and evict it from the cache.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1205)             if self._cache[value]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1206)                 yield self._cache[value].popleft()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1207)             # Otherwise we need to advance the parent iterator to search for
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1208)             # a matching item, caching the rest.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1209)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1210)                 while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1211)                     try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1212)                         item = next(self._it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1213)                     except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1214)                         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1215)                     item_value = self._key(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1216)                     if item_value == value:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1217)                         yield item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1218)                         break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1219)                     elif self._validator(item_value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1220)                         self._cache[item_value].append(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1221) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1222)     def __iter__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1223)         for item in self._it:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1224)             item_value = self._key(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1225)             if self._validator(item_value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1226)                 self._cache[item_value].append(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1227) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1228)         return iter(self._cache)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1229) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1230)     def __getitem__(self, value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1231)         if not self._validator(value):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1232)             return iter(())
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1233) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1234)         return self._get_values(value)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1235) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1236) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1237) def spy(iterable, n=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1238)     """Return a 2-tuple with a list containing the first *n* elements of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1239)     *iterable*, and an iterator with the same items as *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1240)     This allows you to "look ahead" at the items in the iterable without
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1241)     advancing it.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1242) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1243)     There is one item in the list by default:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1244) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1245)         >>> iterable = 'abcdefg'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1246)         >>> head, iterable = spy(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1247)         >>> head
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1248)         ['a']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1249)         >>> list(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1250)         ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd', 'e', 'f', 'g']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1251) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1252)     You may use unpacking to retrieve items instead of lists:
