^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3738) def distinct_combinations(iterable, r):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3739)     """Yield the distinct combinations of *r* items taken from *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3740) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3741)         >>> list(distinct_combinations([0, 0, 1], 2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3742)         [(0, 0), (0, 1)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3743) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3744)     Equivalent to ``set(combinations(iterable))``, except duplicates are not
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3745)     generated and thrown away. For larger input sequences this is much more
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3746)     efficient.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3747) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3748)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3749)     if r < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3750)         raise ValueError('r must be non-negative')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3751)     elif r == 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3752)         yield ()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3753)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3754)     pool = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3755)     generators = [unique_everseen(enumerate(pool), key=itemgetter(1))]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3756)     current_combo = [None] * r
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3757)     level = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3758)     while generators:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3759)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3760)             cur_idx, p = next(generators[-1])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3761)         except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3762)             generators.pop()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3763)             level -= 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3764)             continue
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3765)         current_combo[level] = p
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3766)         if level + 1 == r:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3767)             yield tuple(current_combo)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3768)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3769)             generators.append(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3770)                 unique_everseen(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3771)                     enumerate(pool[cur_idx + 1 :], cur_idx + 1),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3772)                     key=itemgetter(1),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3773)                 )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3774)             )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3775)             level += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3776) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3777) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3778) def filter_except(validator, iterable, *exceptions):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3779)     """Yield the items from *iterable* for which the *validator* function does
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3780)     not raise one of the specified *exceptions*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3781) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3782)     *validator* is called for each item in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3783)     It should be a function that accepts one argument and raises an exception
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3784)     if that item is not valid.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3785) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3786)     >>> iterable = ['1', '2', 'three', '4', None]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3787)     >>> list(filter_except(int, iterable, ValueError, TypeError))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3788)     ['1', '2', '4']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3789) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3790)     If an exception other than one given by *exceptions* is raised by
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3791)     *validator*, it is raised like normal.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3792)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3793)     for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3794)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3795)             validator(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3796)         except exceptions:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3797)             pass
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3798)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3799)             yield item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3800) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3801) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3802) def map_except(function, iterable, *exceptions):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3803)     """Transform each item from *iterable* with *function* and yield the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3804)     result, unless *function* raises one of the specified *exceptions*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3805) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3806)     *function* is called to transform each item in *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3807)     It should accept one argument.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3808) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3809)     >>> iterable = ['1', '2', 'three', '4', None]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3810)     >>> list(map_except(int, iterable, ValueError, TypeError))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3811)     [1, 2, 4]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3812) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3813)     If an exception other than one given by *exceptions* is raised by
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3814)     *function*, it is raised like normal.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3815)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3816)     for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3817)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3818)             yield function(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3819)         except exceptions:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3820)             pass
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3821) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3822) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3823) def map_if(iterable, pred, func, func_else=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3824)     """Evaluate each item from *iterable* using *pred*. If the result is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3825)     equivalent to ``True``, transform the item with *func* and yield it.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3826)     Otherwise, transform the item with *func_else* and yield it.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3827) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3828)     *pred*, *func*, and *func_else* should each be functions that accept
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3829)     one argument. By default, *func_else* is the identity function.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3830) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3831)     >>> from math import sqrt
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3832)     >>> iterable = list(range(-5, 5))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3833)     >>> iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3834)     [-5, -4, -3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3835)     >>> list(map_if(iterable, lambda x: x > 3, lambda x: 'toobig'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3836)     [-5, -4, -3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 'toobig']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3837)     >>> list(map_if(iterable, lambda x: x >= 0,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3838)     ... lambda x: f'{sqrt(x):.2f}', lambda x: None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3839)     [None, None, None, None, None, '0.00', '1.00', '1.41', '1.73', '2.00']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3840)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3841) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3842)     if func_else is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3843)         for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3844)             yield func(item) if pred(item) else item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3845) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3846)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3847)         for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3848)             yield func(item) if pred(item) else func_else(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3849) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3850) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3851) def _sample_unweighted(iterator, k, strict):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3852)     # Algorithm L in the 1994 paper by Kim-Hung Li:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3853)     # "Reservoir-Sampling Algorithms of Time Complexity O(n(1+log(N/n)))".
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3854) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3855)     reservoir = list(islice(iterator, k))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3856)     if strict and len(reservoir) < k:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3857)         raise ValueError('Sample larger than population')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3858)     W = 1.0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3859) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3860)     with suppress(StopIteration):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3861)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3862)             W *= random() ** (1 / k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3863)             skip = floor(log(random()) / log1p(-W))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3864)             element = next(islice(iterator, skip, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3865)             reservoir[randrange(k)] = element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3866) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3867)     shuffle(reservoir)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3868)     return reservoir
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3869) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3870) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3871) def _sample_weighted(iterator, k, weights, strict):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3872)     # Implementation of "A-ExpJ" from the 2006 paper by Efraimidis et al. :
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3873)     # "Weighted random sampling with a reservoir".
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3874) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3875)     # Log-transform for numerical stability for weights that are small/large
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3876)     weight_keys = (log(random()) / weight for weight in weights)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3877) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3878)     # Fill up the reservoir (collection of samples) with the first `k`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3879)     # weight-keys and elements, then heapify the list.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3880)     reservoir = take(k, zip(weight_keys, iterator))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3881)     if strict and len(reservoir) < k:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3882)         raise ValueError('Sample larger than population')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3883) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3884)     heapify(reservoir)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3885) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3886)     # The number of jumps before changing the reservoir is a random variable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3887)     # with an exponential distribution. Sample it using random() and logs.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3888)     smallest_weight_key, _ = reservoir[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3889)     weights_to_skip = log(random()) / smallest_weight_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3890) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3891)     for weight, element in zip(weights, iterator):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3892)         if weight >= weights_to_skip:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3893)             # The notation here is consistent with the paper, but we store
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3894)             # the weight-keys in log-space for better numerical stability.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3895)             smallest_weight_key, _ = reservoir[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3896)             t_w = exp(weight * smallest_weight_key)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3897)             r_2 = uniform(t_w, 1)  # generate U(t_w, 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3898)             weight_key = log(r_2) / weight
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3899)             heapreplace(reservoir, (weight_key, element))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3900)             smallest_weight_key, _ = reservoir[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3901)             weights_to_skip = log(random()) / smallest_weight_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3902)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3903)             weights_to_skip -= weight
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3904) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3905)     ret = [element for weight_key, element in reservoir]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3906)     shuffle(ret)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3907)     return ret
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3908) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3909) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3910) def _sample_counted(population, k, counts, strict):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3911)     element = None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3912)     remaining = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3913) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3914)     def feed(i):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3915)         # Advance *i* steps ahead and consume an element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3916)         nonlocal element, remaining
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3917) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3918)         while i + 1 > remaining:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3919)             i = i - remaining
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3920)             element = next(population)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3921)             remaining = next(counts)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3922)         remaining -= i + 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3923)         return element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3924) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3925)     with suppress(StopIteration):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3926)         reservoir = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3927)         for _ in range(k):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3928)             reservoir.append(feed(0))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3929) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3930)     if strict and len(reservoir) < k:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3931)         raise ValueError('Sample larger than population')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3932) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3933)     with suppress(StopIteration):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3934)         W = 1.0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3935)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3936)             W *= random() ** (1 / k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3937)             skip = floor(log(random()) / log1p(-W))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3938)             element = feed(skip)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3939)             reservoir[randrange(k)] = element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3940) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3941)     shuffle(reservoir)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3942)     return reservoir
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3943) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3944) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3945) def sample(iterable, k, weights=None, *, counts=None, strict=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3946)     """Return a *k*-length list of elements chosen (without replacement)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3947)     from the *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3948) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3949)     Similar to :func:`random.sample`, but works on inputs that aren't
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3950)     indexable (such as sets and dictionaries) and on inputs where the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3951)     size isn't known in advance (such as generators).
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3952) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3953)     >>> iterable = range(100)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3954)     >>> sample(iterable, 5)  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3955)     [81, 60, 96, 16, 4]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3956) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3957)     For iterables with repeated elements, you may supply *counts* to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3958)     indicate the repeats.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3959) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3960)     >>> iterable = ['a', 'b']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3961)     >>> counts = [3, 4]  # Equivalent to 'a', 'a', 'a', 'b', 'b', 'b', 'b'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3962)     >>> sample(iterable, k=3, counts=counts)  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3963)     ['a', 'a', 'b']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3964) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3965)     An iterable with *weights* may be given:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3966) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3967)     >>> iterable = range(100)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3968)     >>> weights = (i * i + 1 for i in range(100))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3969)     >>> sampled = sample(iterable, 5, weights=weights)  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3970)     [79, 67, 74, 66, 78]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3971) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3972)     Weighted selections are made without replacement.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3973)     After an element is selected, it is removed from the pool and the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3974)     relative weights of the other elements increase (this
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3975)     does not match the behavior of :func:`random.sample`'s *counts*
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3976)     parameter). Note that *weights* may not be used with *counts*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3977) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3978)     If the length of *iterable* is less than *k*,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3979)     ``ValueError`` is raised if *strict* is ``True`` and
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3980)     all elements are returned (in shuffled order) if *strict* is ``False``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3981) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3982)     By default, the `Algorithm L <https://w.wiki/ANrM>`__ reservoir sampling
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3983)     technique is used. When *weights* are provided,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3984)     `Algorithm A-ExpJ <https://w.wiki/ANrS>`__ is used instead.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3985) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3986)     Notes on reproducibility:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3987) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3988)     * The algorithms rely on inexact floating-point functions provided
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3989)       by the underlying math library (e.g. ``log``, ``log1p``, and ``pow``).
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3990)       Those functions can `produce slightly different results
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3991)       <https://members.loria.fr/PZimmermann/papers/accuracy.pdf>`_ on
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3992)       different builds.  Accordingly, selections can vary across builds
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3993)       even for the same seed.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3994) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3995)     * The algorithms loop over the input and make selections based on
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3996)       ordinal position, so selections from unordered collections (such as
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3997)       sets) won't reproduce across sessions on the same platform using the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3998)       same seed.  For example, this won't reproduce::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 3999) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4000)           >> seed(8675309)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4001)           >> sample(set('abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz'), 10)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4002)           ['c', 'p', 'e', 'w', 's', 'a', 'j', 'd', 'n', 't']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4003) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4004)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4005)     iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4006) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4007)     if k < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4008)         raise ValueError('k must be non-negative')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4009) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4010)     if k == 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4011)         return []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4012) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4013)     if weights is not None and counts is not None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4014)         raise TypeError('weights and counts are mutually exclusive')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4015) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4016)     elif weights is not None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4017)         weights = iter(weights)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4018)         return _sample_weighted(iterator, k, weights, strict)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4019) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4020)     elif counts is not None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4021)         counts = iter(counts)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4022)         return _sample_counted(iterator, k, counts, strict)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4023) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4024)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4025)         return _sample_unweighted(iterator, k, strict)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4026) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4027) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4028) def is_sorted(iterable, key=None, reverse=False, strict=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4029)     """Returns ``True`` if the items of iterable are in sorted order, and
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4030)     ``False`` otherwise. *key* and *reverse* have the same meaning that they do
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4031)     in the built-in :func:`sorted` function.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4032) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4033)     >>> is_sorted(['1', '2', '3', '4', '5'], key=int)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4034)     True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4035)     >>> is_sorted([5, 4, 3, 1, 2], reverse=True)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4036)     False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4037) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4038)     If *strict*, tests for strict sorting, that is, returns ``False`` if equal
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4039)     elements are found:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4040) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4041)     >>> is_sorted([1, 2, 2])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4042)     True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4043)     >>> is_sorted([1, 2, 2], strict=True)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4044)     False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4045) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4046)     The function returns ``False`` after encountering the first out-of-order
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4047)     item, which means it may produce results that differ from the built-in
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4048)     :func:`sorted` function for objects with unusual comparison dynamics
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4049)     (like ``math.nan``). If there are no out-of-order items, the iterable is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4050)     exhausted.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4051)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4052)     it = iterable if (key is None) else map(key, iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4053)     a, b = tee(it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4054)     next(b, None)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4055)     if reverse:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4056)         b, a = a, b
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4057)     return all(map(lt, a, b)) if strict else not any(map(lt, b, a))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4058)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4060) class AbortThread(BaseException):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4061)     pass

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4064) class callback_iter:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4065)     """Convert a function that uses callbacks to an iterator.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4066) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4067)     .. deprecated:: 11.0.0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4068)        Will be removed in a future major release.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4069) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4070)     Let *func* be a function that takes a `callback` keyword argument.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4071)     For example:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4072) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4073)     >>> def func(callback=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4074)     ...     for i, c in [(1, 'a'), (2, 'b'), (3, 'c')]:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4075)     ...         if callback:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4076)     ...             callback(i, c)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4077)     ...     return 4
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4078) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4079) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4080)     Use ``with callback_iter(func)`` to get an iterator over the parameters
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4081)     that are delivered to the callback.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4082) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4083)     >>> with callback_iter(func) as it:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4084)     ...     for args, kwargs in it:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4085)     ...         print(args)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4086)     (1, 'a')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4087)     (2, 'b')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4088)     (3, 'c')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4089) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4090)     The function will be called in a background thread. The ``done`` property
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4091)     indicates whether it has completed execution.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4092) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4093)     >>> it.done
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4094)     True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4095)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4098)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4100)     4
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4101) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4102)     Notes:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4103) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4104)     * If the function uses some keyword argument besides ``callback``, supply
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4105)       *callback_kwd*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4106)     * If it finished executing, but raised an exception, accessing the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4107)       ``result`` property will raise the same exception.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4108)     * If it hasn't finished executing, accessing the ``result``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4109)       property from within the ``with`` block will raise ``RuntimeError``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4110)     * If it hasn't finished executing, accessing the ``result`` property from
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4111)       outside the ``with`` block will raise a
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4112)       ``more_itertools.AbortThread`` exception.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4113)     * Provide *wait_seconds* to adjust how frequently the it is polled for
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4114)       output.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4115)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4117) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4118)     def __init__(self, func, callback_kwd='callback', wait_seconds=0.1):

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4154)             except self._module.TimeoutError:

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4156)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4158) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4159)     def _reader(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4160)         q = Queue()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4161) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4162)         def callback(*args, **kwargs):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4163)             if self._aborted:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4164)                 raise AbortThread('canceled by user')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4165) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4166)             q.put((args, kwargs))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4167) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4168)         self._future = self._executor.submit(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4169)             self._func, **{self._callback_kwd: callback}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4170)         )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4171) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4172)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4173)             try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4174)                 item = q.get(timeout=self._wait_seconds)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4175)             except Empty:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4176)                 pass
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4177)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4178)                 q.task_done()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4179)                 yield item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4180) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4181)             if self._future.done():
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4182)                 break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4183) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4184)         remaining = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4185)         while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4186)             try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4187)                 item = q.get_nowait()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4188)             except Empty:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4189)                 break

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4191)                 q.task_done()

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4193)         q.join()

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4195) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4196) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4197) def windowed_complete(iterable, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4198)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4199)     Yield ``(beginning, middle, end)`` tuples, where:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4200) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4201)     * Each ``middle`` has *n* items from *iterable*
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4202)     * Each ``beginning`` has the items before the ones in ``middle``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4203)     * Each ``end`` has the items after the ones in ``middle``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4204) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4205)     >>> iterable = range(7)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4206)     >>> n = 3
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4207)     >>> for beginning, middle, end in windowed_complete(iterable, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4208)     ...     print(beginning, middle, end)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4209)     () (0, 1, 2) (3, 4, 5, 6)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4210)     (0,) (1, 2, 3) (4, 5, 6)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4211)     (0, 1) (2, 3, 4) (5, 6)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4212)     (0, 1, 2) (3, 4, 5) (6,)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4213)     (0, 1, 2, 3) (4, 5, 6) ()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4214) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4215)     Note that *n* must be at least 0 and most equal to the length of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4216)     *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4217) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4218)     This function will exhaust the iterable and may require significant
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4219)     storage.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4220)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4221)     if n < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4222)         raise ValueError('n must be >= 0')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4223) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4224)     seq = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4225)     size = len(seq)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4226) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4227)     if n > size:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4228)         raise ValueError('n must be <= len(seq)')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4229) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4230)     for i in range(size - n + 1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4231)         beginning = seq[:i]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4232)         middle = seq[i : i + n]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4233)         end = seq[i + n :]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4234)         yield beginning, middle, end
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4235) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4236) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4237) def all_unique(iterable, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4238)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4239)     Returns ``True`` if all the elements of *iterable* are unique (no two
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4240)     elements are equal).
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4241) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4242)         >>> all_unique('ABCB')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4243)         False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4244) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4245)     If a *key* function is specified, it will be used to make comparisons.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4246) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4247)         >>> all_unique('ABCb')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4248)         True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4249)         >>> all_unique('ABCb', str.lower)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4250)         False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4251) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4252)     The function returns as soon as the first non-unique element is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4253)     encountered. Iterables with a mix of hashable and unhashable items can
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4254)     be used, but the function will be slower for unhashable items.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4255)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4256)     seenset = set()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4257)     seenset_add = seenset.add
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4258)     seenlist = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4259)     seenlist_add = seenlist.append
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4260)     for element in map(key, iterable) if key else iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4261)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4262)             if element in seenset:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4263)                 return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4264)             seenset_add(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4265)         except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4266)             if element in seenlist:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4267)                 return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4268)             seenlist_add(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4269)     return True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4270) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4271) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4272) def nth_product(index, *iterables, repeat=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4273)     """Equivalent to ``list(product(*iterables, repeat=repeat))[index]``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4274) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4275)     The products of *iterables* can be ordered lexicographically.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4276)     :func:`nth_product` computes the product at sort position *index* without
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4277)     computing the previous products.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4278) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4279)         >>> nth_product(8, range(2), range(2), range(2), range(2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4280)         (1, 0, 0, 0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4281) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4282)     The *repeat* keyword argument specifies the number of repetitions
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4283)     of the iterables.  The above example is equivalent to::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4284) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4285)         >>> nth_product(8, range(2), repeat=4)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4286)         (1, 0, 0, 0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4287) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4288)     ``IndexError`` will be raised if the given *index* is invalid.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4289)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4290)     pools = tuple(map(tuple, reversed(iterables))) * repeat
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4291)     ns = tuple(map(len, pools))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4292) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4293)     c = prod(ns)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4294) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4295)     if index < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4296)         index += c
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4297)     if not 0 <= index < c:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4298)         raise IndexError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4299) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4300)     result = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4301)     for pool, n in zip(pools, ns):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4302)         result.append(pool[index % n])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4303)         index //= n
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4304) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4305)     return tuple(reversed(result))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4306) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4307) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4308) def nth_permutation(iterable, r, index):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4309)     """Equivalent to ``list(permutations(iterable, r))[index]```
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4310) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4311)     The subsequences of *iterable* that are of length *r* where order is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4312)     important can be ordered lexicographically. :func:`nth_permutation`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4313)     computes the subsequence at sort position *index* directly, without
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4314)     computing the previous subsequences.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4315) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4316)         >>> nth_permutation('ghijk', 2, 5)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4317)         ('h', 'i')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4318) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4319)     ``ValueError`` will be raised If *r* is negative.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4320)     ``IndexError`` will be raised if the given *index* is invalid.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4321)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4322)     pool = list(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4323)     n = len(pool)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4324)     if r is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4325)         r = n
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4326)     c = perm(n, r)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4327) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4328)     if index < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4329)         index += c
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4330)     if not 0 <= index < c:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4331)         raise IndexError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4332) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4333)     result = [0] * r
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4334)     q = index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4335)     for d in range(n - r + 1, n + 1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4336)         q, i = divmod(q, d)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4337)         result[n - d] = i
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4338)         if q == 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4339)             break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4340) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4341)     return tuple(map(pool.pop, result))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4342) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4343) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4344) def nth_combination_with_replacement(iterable, r, index):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4345)     """Equivalent to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4346)     ``list(combinations_with_replacement(iterable, r))[index]``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4347) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4348) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4349)     The subsequences with repetition of *iterable* that are of length *r* can
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4350)     be ordered lexicographically. :func:`nth_combination_with_replacement`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4351)     computes the subsequence at sort position *index* directly, without
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4352)     computing the previous subsequences with replacement.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4353) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4354)         >>> nth_combination_with_replacement(range(5), 3, 5)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4355)         (0, 1, 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4356) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4357)     ``ValueError`` will be raised If *r* is negative.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4358)     ``IndexError`` will be raised if the given *index* is invalid.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4359)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4360)     pool = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4361)     n = len(pool)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4362)     if r < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4363)         raise ValueError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4364)     c = comb(n + r - 1, r) if n else 0 if r else 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4365) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4366)     if index < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4367)         index += c
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4368)     if not 0 <= index < c:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4369)         raise IndexError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4370) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4371)     result = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4372)     i = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4373)     while r:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4374)         r -= 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4375)         while n >= 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4376)             num_combs = comb(n + r - 1, r)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4377)             if index < num_combs:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4378)                 break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4379)             n -= 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4380)             i += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4381)             index -= num_combs
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4382)         result.append(pool[i])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4383) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4384)     return tuple(result)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4385) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4386) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4387) def value_chain(*args):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4388)     """Yield all arguments passed to the function in the same order in which
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4389)     they were passed. If an argument itself is iterable then iterate over its
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4390)     values.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4391) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4392)         >>> list(value_chain(1, 2, 3, [4, 5, 6]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4393)         [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4394) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4395)     Binary and text strings are not considered iterable and are emitted
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4396)     as-is:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4397) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4398)         >>> list(value_chain('12', '34', ['56', '78']))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4399)         ['12', '34', '56', '78']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4400) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4401)     Pre- or postpend a single element to an iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4402) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4403)         >>> list(value_chain(1, [2, 3, 4, 5, 6]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4404)         [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4405)         >>> list(value_chain([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], 6))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4406)         [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4407) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4408)     Multiple levels of nesting are not flattened.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4409) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4410)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4411)     scalar_types = (str, bytes)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4412)     for value in args:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4413)         if isinstance(value, scalar_types):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4414)             yield value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4415)             continue
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4416)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4417)             yield from value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4418)         except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4419)             yield value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4420) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4421) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4422) def product_index(element, *iterables, repeat=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4423)     """Equivalent to ``list(product(*iterables, repeat=repeat)).index(tuple(element))``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4424) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4425)     The products of *iterables* can be ordered lexicographically.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4426)     :func:`product_index` computes the first index of *element* without
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4427)     computing the previous products.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4428) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4429)         >>> product_index([8, 2], range(10), range(5))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4430)         42
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4431) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4432)     The *repeat* keyword argument specifies the number of repetitions
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4433)     of the iterables::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4434) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4435)         >>> product_index([8, 0, 7], range(10), repeat=3)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4436)         807
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4437) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4438)     ``ValueError`` will be raised if the given *element* isn't in the product
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4439)     of *args*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4440)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4441)     elements = tuple(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4442)     pools = tuple(map(tuple, iterables)) * repeat
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4443)     if len(elements) != len(pools):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4444)         raise ValueError('element is not a product of args')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4445) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4446)     index = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4447)     for elem, pool in zip(elements, pools):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4448)         index = index * len(pool) + pool.index(elem)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4449)     return index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4450) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4451) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4452) def combination_index(element, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4453)     """Equivalent to ``list(combinations(iterable, r)).index(element)``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4454) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4455)     The subsequences of *iterable* that are of length *r* can be ordered
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4456)     lexicographically. :func:`combination_index` computes the index of the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4457)     first *element*, without computing the previous combinations.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4458) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4459)         >>> combination_index('adf', 'abcdefg')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4460)         10
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4461) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4462)     ``ValueError`` will be raised if the given *element* isn't one of the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4463)     combinations of *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4464)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4465)     element = enumerate(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4466)     k, y = next(element, (None, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4467)     if k is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4468)         return 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4469) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4470)     indexes = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4471)     pool = enumerate(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4472)     for n, x in pool:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4473)         if x == y:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4474)             indexes.append(n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4475)             tmp, y = next(element, (None, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4476)             if tmp is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4477)                 break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4478)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4479)                 k = tmp
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4480)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4481)         raise ValueError('element is not a combination of iterable')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4482) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4483)     n, _ = last(pool, default=(n, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4484) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4485)     index = 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4486)     for i, j in enumerate(reversed(indexes), start=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4487)         j = n - j
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4488)         if i <= j:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4489)             index += comb(j, i)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4490) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4491)     return comb(n + 1, k + 1) - index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4492) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4493) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4494) def combination_with_replacement_index(element, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4495)     """Equivalent to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4496)     ``list(combinations_with_replacement(iterable, r)).index(element)``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4497) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4498)     The subsequences with repetition of *iterable* that are of length *r* can
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4499)     be ordered lexicographically. :func:`combination_with_replacement_index`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4500)     computes the index of the first *element*, without computing the previous
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4501)     combinations with replacement.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4502) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4503)         >>> combination_with_replacement_index('adf', 'abcdefg')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4504)         20
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4505) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4506)     ``ValueError`` will be raised if the given *element* isn't one of the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4507)     combinations with replacement of *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4508)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4509)     element = tuple(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4510)     l = len(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4511)     element = enumerate(element)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4512) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4513)     k, y = next(element, (None, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4514)     if k is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4515)         return 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4516) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4517)     indexes = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4518)     pool = tuple(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4519)     for n, x in enumerate(pool):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4520)         while x == y:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4521)             indexes.append(n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4522)             tmp, y = next(element, (None, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4523)             if tmp is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4524)                 break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4525)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4526)                 k = tmp
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4527)         if y is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4528)             break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4529)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4530)         raise ValueError(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4531)             'element is not a combination with replacement of iterable'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4532)         )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4533) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4534)     n = len(pool)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4535)     occupations = [0] * n
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4536)     for p in indexes:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4537)         occupations[p] += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4538) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4539)     index = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4540)     cumulative_sum = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4541)     for k in range(1, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4542)         cumulative_sum += occupations[k - 1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4543)         j = l + n - 1 - k - cumulative_sum
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4544)         i = n - k
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4545)         if i <= j:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4546)             index += comb(j, i)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4547) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4548)     return index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4549) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4550) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4551) def permutation_index(element, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4552)     """Equivalent to ``list(permutations(iterable, r)).index(element)```
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4553) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4554)     The subsequences of *iterable* that are of length *r* where order is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4555)     important can be ordered lexicographically. :func:`permutation_index`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4556)     computes the index of the first *element* directly, without computing
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4557)     the previous permutations.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4558) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4559)         >>> permutation_index([1, 3, 2], range(5))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4560)         19
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4561) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4562)     ``ValueError`` will be raised if the given *element* isn't one of the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4563)     permutations of *iterable*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4564)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4565)     index = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4566)     pool = list(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4567)     for i, x in zip(range(len(pool), -1, -1), element):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4568)         r = pool.index(x)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4569)         index = index * i + r
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4570)         del pool[r]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4571) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4572)     return index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4573) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4574) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4575) class countable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4576)     """Wrap *iterable* and keep a count of how many items have been consumed.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4577) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4578)     The ``items_seen`` attribute starts at ``0`` and increments as the iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4579)     is consumed:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4580) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4581)         >>> iterable = map(str, range(10))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4582)         >>> it = countable(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4583)         >>> it.items_seen
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4584)         0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4585)         >>> next(it), next(it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4586)         ('0', '1')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4587)         >>> list(it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4588)         ['2', '3', '4', '5', '6', '7', '8', '9']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4589)         >>> it.items_seen
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4590)         10
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4591)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4592) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4593)     def __init__(self, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4594)         self._iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4595)         self.items_seen = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4596) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4597)     def __iter__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4598)         return self
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4599) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4600)     def __next__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4601)         item = next(self._iterator)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4602)         self.items_seen += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4603) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4604)         return item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4605) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4606) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4607) def chunked_even(iterable, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4608)     """Break *iterable* into lists of approximately length *n*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4609)     Items are distributed such the lengths of the lists differ by at most
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4610)     1 item.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4611) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4612)     >>> iterable = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4613)     >>> n = 3
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4614)     >>> list(chunked_even(iterable, n))  # List lengths: 3, 2, 2
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4615)     [[1, 2, 3], [4, 5], [6, 7]]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4616)     >>> list(chunked(iterable, n))  # List lengths: 3, 3, 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4617)     [[1, 2, 3], [4, 5, 6], [7]]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4618) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4619)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4620)     iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4621) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4622)     # Initialize a buffer to process the chunks while keeping
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4623)     # some back to fill any underfilled chunks
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4624)     min_buffer = (n - 1) * (n - 2)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4625)     buffer = list(islice(iterator, min_buffer))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4626) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4627)     # Append items until we have a completed chunk
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4628)     for _ in islice(map(buffer.append, iterator), n, None, n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4629)         yield buffer[:n]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4630)         del buffer[:n]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4631) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4632)     # Check if any chunks need addition processing
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4633)     if not buffer:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4634)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4635)     length = len(buffer)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4636) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4637)     # Chunks are either size `full_size <= n` or `partial_size = full_size - 1`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4638)     q, r = divmod(length, n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4639)     num_lists = q + (1 if r > 0 else 0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4640)     q, r = divmod(length, num_lists)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4641)     full_size = q + (1 if r > 0 else 0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4642)     partial_size = full_size - 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4643)     num_full = length - partial_size * num_lists
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4644) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4645)     # Yield chunks of full size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4646)     partial_start_idx = num_full * full_size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4647)     if full_size > 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4648)         for i in range(0, partial_start_idx, full_size):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4649)             yield buffer[i : i + full_size]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4650) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4651)     # Yield chunks of partial size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4652)     if partial_size > 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4653)         for i in range(partial_start_idx, length, partial_size):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4654)             yield buffer[i : i + partial_size]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4655) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4656) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4657) def zip_broadcast(*objects, scalar_types=(str, bytes), strict=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4658)     """A version of :func:`zip` that "broadcasts" any scalar
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4659)     (i.e., non-iterable) items into output tuples.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4660) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4661)     >>> iterable_1 = [1, 2, 3]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4662)     >>> iterable_2 = ['a', 'b', 'c']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4663)     >>> scalar = '_'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4664)     >>> list(zip_broadcast(iterable_1, iterable_2, scalar))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4665)     [(1, 'a', '_'), (2, 'b', '_'), (3, 'c', '_')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4666) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4667)     The *scalar_types* keyword argument determines what types are considered
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4668)     scalar. It is set to ``(str, bytes)`` by default. Set it to ``None`` to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4669)     treat strings and byte strings as iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4670) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4671)     >>> list(zip_broadcast('abc', 0, 'xyz', scalar_types=None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4672)     [('a', 0, 'x'), ('b', 0, 'y'), ('c', 0, 'z')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4673) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4674)     If the *strict* keyword argument is ``True``, then
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4675)     ``ValueError`` will be raised if any of the iterables have
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4676)     different lengths.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4677)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4678) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4679)     def is_scalar(obj):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4680)         if scalar_types and isinstance(obj, scalar_types):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4681)             return True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4682)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4683)             iter(obj)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4684)         except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4685)             return True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4686)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4687)             return False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4688) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4689)     size = len(objects)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4690)     if not size:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4691)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4692) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4693)     new_item = [None] * size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4694)     iterables, iterable_positions = [], []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4695)     for i, obj in enumerate(objects):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4696)         if is_scalar(obj):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4697)             new_item[i] = obj
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4698)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4699)             iterables.append(iter(obj))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4700)             iterable_positions.append(i)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4701) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4702)     if not iterables:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4703)         yield tuple(objects)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4704)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4705) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4706)     for item in zip(*iterables, strict=strict):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4707)         for i, new_item[i] in zip(iterable_positions, item):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4708)             pass
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4709)         yield tuple(new_item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4710) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4711) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4712) def unique_in_window(iterable, n, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4713)     """Yield the items from *iterable* that haven't been seen recently.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4714)     *n* is the size of the sliding window.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4715) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4716)         >>> iterable = [0, 1, 0, 2, 3, 0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4717)         >>> n = 3
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4718)         >>> list(unique_in_window(iterable, n))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4719)         [0, 1, 2, 3, 0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4720) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4721)     The *key* function, if provided, will be used to determine uniqueness:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4722) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4723)         >>> list(unique_in_window('abAcda', 3, key=lambda x: x.lower()))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4724)         ['a', 'b', 'c', 'd', 'a']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4725) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4726)     Updates a sliding window no larger than n and yields a value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4727)     if the item only occurs once in the updated window.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4728) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4729)     When `n == 1`, *unique_in_window* is memoryless:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4730) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4731)         >>> list(unique_in_window('aab', n=1))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4732)         ['a', 'a', 'b']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4733) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4734)     The items in *iterable* must be hashable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4735) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4736)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4737)     if n <= 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4738)         raise ValueError('n must be greater than 0')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4739) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4740)     window = deque(maxlen=n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4741)     counts = Counter()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4742)     use_key = key is not None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4743) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4744)     for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4745)         if len(window) == n:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4746)             to_discard = window[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4747)             if counts[to_discard] == 1:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4748)                 del counts[to_discard]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4749)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4750)                 counts[to_discard] -= 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4751) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4752)         k = key(item) if use_key else item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4753)         if k not in counts:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4754)             yield item
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4755)         counts[k] += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4756)         window.append(k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4757) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4758) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4759) def duplicates_everseen(iterable, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4760)     """Yield duplicate elements after their first appearance.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4761) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4762)     >>> list(duplicates_everseen('mississippi'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4763)     ['s', 'i', 's', 's', 'i', 'p', 'i']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4764)     >>> list(duplicates_everseen('AaaBbbCccAaa', str.lower))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4765)     ['a', 'a', 'b', 'b', 'c', 'c', 'A', 'a', 'a']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4766) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4767)     This function is analogous to :func:`unique_everseen` and is subject to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4768)     the same performance considerations.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4769) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4770)     If you would like each duplicate to only appear once, much like ``uniq -d``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4771)     in the Unix shell or ``Itertools::duplicates`` from the Rust ``itertools``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4772)     crate, pass the return value of this function into :func:`unique_everseen`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4773)     with the same ``key``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4774) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4775)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4776)     seen_set = set()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4777)     seen_list = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4778)     use_key = key is not None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4779) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4780)     for element in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4781)         k = key(element) if use_key else element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4782)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4783)             if k not in seen_set:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4784)                 seen_set.add(k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4785)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4786)                 yield element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4787)         except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4788)             if k not in seen_list:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4789)                 seen_list.append(k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4790)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4791)                 yield element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4792) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4793) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4794) def duplicates_justseen(iterable, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4795)     """Yields serially-duplicate elements after their first appearance.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4796) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4797)     >>> list(duplicates_justseen('mississippi'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4798)     ['s', 's', 'p']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4799)     >>> list(duplicates_justseen('AaaBbbCccAaa', str.lower))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4800)     ['a', 'a', 'b', 'b', 'c', 'c', 'a', 'a']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4801) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4802)     This function is analogous to :func:`unique_justseen`.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4803) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4804)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4805)     return flatten(g for _, g in groupby(iterable, key) for _ in g)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4806) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4807)
