^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4808) def classify_unique(iterable, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4809)     """Classify each element in terms of its uniqueness.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4810) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4811)     For each element in the input iterable, return a 3-tuple consisting of:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4812) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4813)     1. The element itself
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4814)     2. ``False`` if the element is equal to the one preceding it in the input,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4815)        ``True`` otherwise (i.e. the equivalent of :func:`unique_justseen`)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4816)     3. ``False`` if this element has been seen anywhere in the input before,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4817)        ``True`` otherwise (i.e. the equivalent of :func:`unique_everseen`)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4818) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4819)     >>> list(classify_unique('otto'))    # doctest: +NORMALIZE_WHITESPACE
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4820)     [('o', True,  True),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4821)      ('t', True,  True),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4822)      ('t', False, False),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4823)      ('o', True,  False)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4824) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4825)     This function is analogous to :func:`unique_everseen` and is subject to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4826)     the same performance considerations.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4827) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4828)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4829)     seen_set = set()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4830)     seen_list = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4831)     use_key = key is not None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4832)     previous = None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4833) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4834)     for i, element in enumerate(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4835)         k = key(element) if use_key else element
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4836)         is_unique_justseen = not i or previous != k
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4837)         previous = k
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4838)         is_unique_everseen = False
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4839)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4840)             if k not in seen_set:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4841)                 seen_set.add(k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4842)                 is_unique_everseen = True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4843)         except TypeError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4844)             if k not in seen_list:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4845)                 seen_list.append(k)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4846)                 is_unique_everseen = True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4847)         yield element, is_unique_justseen, is_unique_everseen
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4848) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4849) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4850) def minmax(iterable_or_value, *others, key=None, default=_marker):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4851)     """Returns both the smallest and largest items from an iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4852)     or from two or more arguments.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4853) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4854)         >>> minmax([3, 1, 5])
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4855)         (1, 5)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4856) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4857)         >>> minmax(4, 2, 6)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4858)         (2, 6)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4859) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4860)     If a *key* function is provided, it will be used to transform the input
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4861)     items for comparison.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4862) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4863)         >>> minmax([5, 30], key=str)  # '30' sorts before '5'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4864)         (30, 5)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4865) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4866)     If a *default* value is provided, it will be returned if there are no
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4867)     input items.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4868) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4869)         >>> minmax([], default=(0, 0))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4870)         (0, 0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4871) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4872)     Otherwise ``ValueError`` is raised.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4873) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4874)     This function makes a single pass over the input elements and takes care to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4875)     minimize the number of comparisons made during processing.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4876) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4877)     Note that unlike the builtin ``max`` function, which always returns the first
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4878)     item with the maximum value, this function may return another item when there are
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4879)     ties.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4880) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4881)     This function is based on the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4882)     `recipe <https://code.activestate.com/recipes/577916-fast-minmax-function>`__ by
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4883)     Raymond Hettinger.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4884)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4885)     iterable = (iterable_or_value, *others) if others else iterable_or_value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4886) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4887)     it = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4888) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4889)     try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4890)         lo = hi = next(it)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4891)     except StopIteration as exc:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4892)         if default is _marker:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4893)             raise ValueError(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4894)                 '`minmax()` argument is an empty iterable. '
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4895)                 'Provide a `default` value to suppress this error.'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4896)             ) from exc
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4897)         return default
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4898) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4899)     # Different branches depending on the presence of key. This saves a lot
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4900)     # of unimportant copies which would slow the "key=None" branch
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4901)     # significantly down.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4902)     if key is None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4903)         for x, y in zip_longest(it, it, fillvalue=lo):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4904)             if y < x:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4905)                 if y < lo:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4906)                     lo = y
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4907)                 if hi < x:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4908)                     hi = x
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4909)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4910)                 if x < lo:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4911)                     lo = x
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4912)                 if hi < y:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4913)                     hi = y
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4914) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4915)     else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4916)         lo_key = hi_key = key(lo)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4917) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4918)         for x, y in zip_longest(it, it, fillvalue=lo):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4919)             x_key, y_key = key(x), key(y)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4920) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4921)             if y_key < x_key:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4922)                 if y_key < lo_key:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4923)                     lo, lo_key = y, y_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4924)                 if hi_key < x_key:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4925)                     hi, hi_key = x, x_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4926)             else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4927)                 if x_key < lo_key:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4928)                     lo, lo_key = x, x_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4929)                 if hi_key < y_key:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4930)                     hi, hi_key = y, y_key
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4931) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4932)     return lo, hi
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4933) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4934) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4935) def constrained_batches(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4936)     iterable, max_size, max_count=None, get_len=len, strict=True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4937) ):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4938)     """Yield batches of items from *iterable* with a combined size limited by
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4939)     *max_size*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4940) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4941)     >>> iterable = [b'12345', b'123', b'12345678', b'1', b'1', b'12', b'1']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4942)     >>> list(constrained_batches(iterable, 10))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4943)     [(b'12345', b'123'), (b'12345678', b'1', b'1'), (b'12', b'1')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4944) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4945)     If a *max_count* is supplied, the number of items per batch is also
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4946)     limited:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4947) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4948)     >>> iterable = [b'12345', b'123', b'12345678', b'1', b'1', b'12', b'1']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4949)     >>> list(constrained_batches(iterable, 10, max_count = 2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4950)     [(b'12345', b'123'), (b'12345678', b'1'), (b'1', b'12'), (b'1',)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4951) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4952)     If a *get_len* function is supplied, use that instead of :func:`len` to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4953)     determine item size.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4954) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4955)     If *strict* is ``True``, raise ``ValueError`` if any single item is bigger
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4956)     than *max_size*. Otherwise, allow single items to exceed *max_size*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4957)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4958)     if max_size <= 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4959)         raise ValueError('maximum size must be greater than zero')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4960) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4961)     batch = []
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4962)     batch_size = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4963)     batch_count = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4964)     for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4965)         item_len = get_len(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4966)         if strict and item_len > max_size:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4967)             raise ValueError('item size exceeds maximum size')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4968) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4969)         reached_count = batch_count == max_count
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4970)         reached_size = item_len + batch_size > max_size
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4971)         if batch_count and (reached_size or reached_count):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4972)             yield tuple(batch)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4973)             batch.clear()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4974)             batch_size = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4975)             batch_count = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4976) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4977)         batch.append(item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4978)         batch_size += item_len
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4979)         batch_count += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4980) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4981)     if batch:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4982)         yield tuple(batch)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4983) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4984) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4985) def gray_product(*iterables, repeat=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4986)     """Like :func:`itertools.product`, but return tuples in an order such
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4987)     that only one element in the generated tuple changes from one iteration
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4988)     to the next.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4989) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4990)         >>> list(gray_product('AB','CD'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4991)         [('A', 'C'), ('B', 'C'), ('B', 'D'), ('A', 'D')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4992) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4993)     The *repeat* keyword argument specifies the number of repetitions
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4994)     of the iterables.  For example, ``gray_product('AB', repeat=3)`` is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4995)     equivalent to ``gray_product('AB', 'AB', 'AB')``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4996) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4997)     This function consumes all of the input iterables before producing output.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4998)     If any of the input iterables have fewer than two items, ``ValueError``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 4999)     is raised.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5000) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5001)     For information on the algorithm, see
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5002)     `this section <https://www-cs-faculty.stanford.edu/~knuth/fasc2a.ps.gz>`__
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5003)     of Donald Knuth's *The Art of Computer Programming*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5004)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5005)     all_iterables = tuple(map(tuple, iterables)) * repeat
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5006)     iterable_count = len(all_iterables)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5007)     for iterable in all_iterables:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5008)         if len(iterable) < 2:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5009)             raise ValueError("each iterable must have two or more items")
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5010) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5011)     # This is based on "Algorithm H" from section 7.2.1.1, page 20.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5012)     # a holds the indexes of the source iterables for the n-tuple to be yielded
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5013)     # f is the array of "focus pointers"
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5014)     # o is the array of "directions"
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5015)     a = [0] * iterable_count
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5016)     f = list(range(iterable_count + 1))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5017)     o = [1] * iterable_count
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5018)     while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5019)         yield tuple(all_iterables[i][a[i]] for i in range(iterable_count))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5020)         j = f[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5021)         f[0] = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5022)         if j == iterable_count:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5023)             break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5024)         a[j] = a[j] + o[j]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5025)         if a[j] == 0 or a[j] == len(all_iterables[j]) - 1:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5026)             o[j] = -o[j]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5027)             f[j] = f[j + 1]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5028)             f[j + 1] = j + 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5029) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5030) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5031) def partial_product(*iterables, repeat=1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5032)     """Yields tuples containing one item from each iterator, with subsequent
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5033)     tuples changing a single item at a time by advancing each iterator until it
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5034)     is exhausted. This sequence guarantees every value in each iterable is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5035)     output at least once without generating all possible combinations.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5036) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5037)     This may be useful, for example, when testing an expensive function.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5038) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5039)         >>> list(partial_product('AB', 'C', 'DEF'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5040)         [('A', 'C', 'D'), ('B', 'C', 'D'), ('B', 'C', 'E'), ('B', 'C', 'F')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5041) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5042)     The *repeat* keyword argument specifies the number of repetitions
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5043)     of the iterables.  For example, ``partial_product('AB', repeat=3)`` is
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5044)     equivalent to ``partial_product('AB', 'AB', 'AB')``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5045)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5046) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5047)     all_iterables = tuple(map(tuple, iterables)) * repeat
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5048)     iterators = tuple(map(iter, all_iterables))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5049) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5050)     try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5051)         prod = [next(it) for it in iterators]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5052)     except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5053)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5054)     yield tuple(prod)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5055) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5056)     for i, it in enumerate(iterators):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5057)         for prod[i] in it:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5058)             yield tuple(prod)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5059) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5060) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5061) def takewhile_inclusive(predicate, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5062)     """A variant of :func:`takewhile` that yields one additional element.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5063) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5064)         >>> list(takewhile_inclusive(lambda x: x < 5, [1, 4, 6, 4, 1]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5065)         [1, 4, 6]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5066) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5067)     :func:`takewhile` would return ``[1, 4]``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5068)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5069)     for x in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5070)         yield x
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5071)         if not predicate(x):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5072)             break
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5073) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5074) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5075) def outer_product(func, xs, ys, *args, **kwargs):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5076)     """A generalized outer product that applies a binary function to all
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5077)     pairs of items. Returns a 2D matrix with ``len(xs)`` rows and ``len(ys)``
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5078)     columns.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5079)     Also accepts ``*args`` and ``**kwargs`` that are passed to ``func``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5080) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5081)     Multiplication table:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5082) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5083)     >>> from operator import mul
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5084)     >>> list(outer_product(mul, range(1, 4), range(1, 6)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5085)     [(1, 2, 3, 4, 5), (2, 4, 6, 8, 10), (3, 6, 9, 12, 15)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5086) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5087)     Cross tabulation:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5088) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5089)     >>> xs = ['A', 'B', 'A', 'A', 'B', 'B', 'A', 'A', 'B', 'B']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5090)     >>> ys = ['X', 'X', 'X', 'Y', 'Z', 'Z', 'Y', 'Y', 'Z', 'Z']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5091)     >>> pair_counts = Counter(zip(xs, ys))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5092)     >>> count_rows = lambda x, y: pair_counts[x, y]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5093)     >>> list(outer_product(count_rows, sorted(set(xs)), sorted(set(ys))))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5094)     [(2, 3, 0), (1, 0, 4)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5095) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5096)     Usage with ``*args`` and ``**kwargs``:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5097) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5098)     >>> animals = ['cat', 'wolf', 'mouse']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5099)     >>> list(outer_product(min, animals, animals, key=len))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5100)     [('cat', 'cat', 'cat'), ('cat', 'wolf', 'wolf'), ('cat', 'wolf', 'mouse')]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5101)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5102)     ys = tuple(ys)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5103)     return batched(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5104)         starmap(lambda x, y: func(x, y, *args, **kwargs), product(xs, ys)),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5105)         n=len(ys),
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5106)     )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5107) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5108) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5109) def iter_suppress(iterable, *exceptions):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5110)     """Yield each of the items from *iterable*. If the iteration raises one of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5111)     the specified *exceptions*, that exception will be suppressed and iteration
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5112)     will stop.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5113) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5114)     >>> from itertools import chain
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5115)     >>> def breaks_at_five(x):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5116)     ...     while True:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5117)     ...         if x >= 5:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5118)     ...             raise RuntimeError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5119)     ...         yield x
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5120)     ...         x += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5121)     >>> it_1 = iter_suppress(breaks_at_five(1), RuntimeError)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5122)     >>> it_2 = iter_suppress(breaks_at_five(2), RuntimeError)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5123)     >>> list(chain(it_1, it_2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5124)     [1, 2, 3, 4, 2, 3, 4]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5125)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5126)     try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5127)         yield from iterable
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5128)     except exceptions:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5129)         return
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5130) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5131) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5132) def filter_map(func, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5133)     """Apply *func* to every element of *iterable*, yielding only those which
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5134)     are not ``None``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5135) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5136)     >>> elems = ['1', 'a', '2', 'b', '3']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5137)     >>> list(filter_map(lambda s: int(s) if s.isnumeric() else None, elems))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5138)     [1, 2, 3]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5139)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5140)     for x in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5141)         y = func(x)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5142)         if y is not None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5143)             yield y
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5144) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5145) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5146) def powerset_of_sets(iterable, *, baseset=set):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5147)     """Yields all possible subsets of the iterable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5148) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5149)         >>> list(powerset_of_sets([1, 2, 3]))  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5150)         [set(), {1}, {2}, {3}, {1, 2}, {1, 3}, {2, 3}, {1, 2, 3}]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5151)         >>> list(powerset_of_sets([1, 1, 0]))  # doctest: +SKIP
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5152)         [set(), {1}, {0}, {0, 1}]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5153) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5154)     :func:`powerset_of_sets` takes care to minimize the number
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5155)     of hash operations performed.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5156) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5157)     The *baseset* parameter determines what kind of sets are
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5158)     constructed, either *set* or *frozenset*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5159)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5160)     sets = tuple(dict.fromkeys(map(frozenset, zip(iterable))))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5161)     union = baseset().union
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5162)     return chain.from_iterable(
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5163)         starmap(union, combinations(sets, r)) for r in range(len(sets) + 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5164)     )
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5165) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5166) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5167) def join_mappings(**field_to_map):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5168)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5169)     Joins multiple mappings together using their common keys.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5170) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5171)     >>> user_scores = {'elliot': 50, 'claris': 60}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5172)     >>> user_times = {'elliot': 30, 'claris': 40}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5173)     >>> join_mappings(score=user_scores, time=user_times)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5174)     {'elliot': {'score': 50, 'time': 30}, 'claris': {'score': 60, 'time': 40}}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5175)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5176)     ret = defaultdict(dict)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5177) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5178)     for field_name, mapping in field_to_map.items():
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5179)         for key, value in mapping.items():
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5180)             ret[key][field_name] = value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5181) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5182)     return dict(ret)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5183) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5184) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5185) def _complex_sumprod(v1, v2):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5186)     """High precision sumprod() for complex numbers.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5187)     Used by :func:`dft` and :func:`idft`.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5188)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5189) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5190)     real = attrgetter('real')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5191)     imag = attrgetter('imag')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5192)     r1 = chain(map(real, v1), map(neg, map(imag, v1)))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5193)     r2 = chain(map(real, v2), map(imag, v2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5194)     i1 = chain(map(real, v1), map(imag, v1))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5195)     i2 = chain(map(imag, v2), map(real, v2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5196)     return complex(_fsumprod(r1, r2), _fsumprod(i1, i2))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5197) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5198) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5199) def dft(xarr):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5200)     """Discrete Fourier Transform. *xarr* is a sequence of complex numbers.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5201)     Yields the components of the corresponding transformed output vector.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5202) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5203)     >>> import cmath
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5204)     >>> xarr = [1, 2-1j, -1j, -1+2j]  # time domain
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5205)     >>> Xarr = [2, -2-2j, -2j, 4+4j]  # frequency domain
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5206)     >>> magnitudes, phases = zip(*map(cmath.polar, Xarr))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5207)     >>> all(map(cmath.isclose, dft(xarr), Xarr))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5208)     True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5209) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5210)     Inputs are restricted to numeric types that can add and multiply
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5211)     with a complex number.  This includes int, float, complex, and
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5212)     Fraction, but excludes Decimal.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5213) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5214)     See :func:`idft` for the inverse Discrete Fourier Transform.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5215)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5216)     N = len(xarr)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5217)     roots_of_unity = [e ** (n / N * tau * -1j) for n in range(N)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5218)     for k in range(N):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5219)         coeffs = [roots_of_unity[k * n % N] for n in range(N)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5220)         yield _complex_sumprod(xarr, coeffs)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5221) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5222) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5223) def idft(Xarr):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5224)     """Inverse Discrete Fourier Transform. *Xarr* is a sequence of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5225)     complex numbers. Yields the components of the corresponding
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5226)     inverse-transformed output vector.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5227) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5228)     >>> import cmath
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5229)     >>> xarr = [1, 2-1j, -1j, -1+2j]  # time domain
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5230)     >>> Xarr = [2, -2-2j, -2j, 4+4j]  # frequency domain
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5231)     >>> all(map(cmath.isclose, idft(Xarr), xarr))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5232)     True
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5233) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5234)     Inputs are restricted to numeric types that can add and multiply
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5235)     with a complex number.  This includes int, float, complex, and
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5236)     Fraction, but excludes Decimal.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5237) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5238)     See :func:`dft` for the Discrete Fourier Transform.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5239)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5240)     N = len(Xarr)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5241)     roots_of_unity = [e ** (n / N * tau * 1j) for n in range(N)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5242)     for k in range(N):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5243)         coeffs = [roots_of_unity[k * n % N] for n in range(N)]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5244)         yield _complex_sumprod(Xarr, coeffs) / N
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5245) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5246) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5247) def doublestarmap(func, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5248)     """Apply *func* to every item of *iterable* by dictionary unpacking
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5249)     the item into *func*.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5250) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5251)     The difference between :func:`itertools.starmap` and :func:`doublestarmap`
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5252)     parallels the distinction between ``func(*a)`` and ``func(**a)``.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5253) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5254)     >>> iterable = [{'a': 1, 'b': 2}, {'a': 40, 'b': 60}]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5255)     >>> list(doublestarmap(lambda a, b: a + b, iterable))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5256)     [3, 100]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5257) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5258)     ``TypeError`` will be raised if *func*'s signature doesn't match the
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5259)     mapping contained in *iterable* or if *iterable* does not contain mappings.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5260)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5261)     for item in iterable:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5262)         yield func(**item)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5263) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5264) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5265) def _nth_prime_bounds(n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5266)     """Bounds for the nth prime (counting from 1): lb < p_n < ub."""
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5267)     # At and above 688,383, the lb/ub spread is under 0.003 * p_n.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5268) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5269)     if n < 1:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5270)         raise ValueError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5271) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5272)     if n < 6:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5273)         return (n, 2.25 * n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5274) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5275)     # https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime-counting_function#Inequalities
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5276)     upper_bound = n * log(n * log(n))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5277)     lower_bound = upper_bound - n
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5278)     if n >= 688_383:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5279)         upper_bound -= n * (1.0 - (log(log(n)) - 2.0) / log(n))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5280) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5281)     return lower_bound, upper_bound
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5282) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5283) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5284) def nth_prime(n, *, approximate=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5285)     """Return the nth prime (counting from 0).
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5286) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5287)     >>> nth_prime(0)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5288)     2
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5289)     >>> nth_prime(100)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5290)     547
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5291) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5292)     If *approximate* is set to True, will return a prime close
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5293)     to the nth prime.  The estimation is much faster than computing
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5294)     an exact result.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5295) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5296)     >>> nth_prime(200_000_000, approximate=True)  # Exact result is 4222234763
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5297)     4217820427
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5298) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5299)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5300)     lb, ub = _nth_prime_bounds(n + 1)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5301) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5302)     if not approximate or n <= 1_000_000:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5303)         return nth(sieve(ceil(ub)), n)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5304) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5305)     # Search from the midpoint and return the first odd prime
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5306)     odd = floor((lb + ub) / 2) | 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5307)     return first_true(count(odd, step=2), pred=is_prime)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5308) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5309) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5310) def argmin(iterable, *, key=None):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5311)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5312)     Index of the first occurrence of a minimum value in an iterable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5313)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5317)         3
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5318) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5319)     For example, look up a label corresponding to the position
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5320)     of a value that minimizes a cost function::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5321) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5322)         >>> def cost(x):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5323)         ...     "Days for a wound to heal given a subject's age."

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5330)         >>> labels[argmin(ages, key=cost)]

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5334)         >>> min(ages, key=cost)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5335)         10
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5336) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5337)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5338)     if key is not None:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5339)         iterable = map(key, iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5340)     return min(enumerate(iterable), key=itemgetter(1))[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5341) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5342)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5352)     For example, identify the best machine learning model::

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5355)         >>> accuracy = [  68,        61,          84,       72      ]

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5367)         iterable = map(key, iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5368)     return max(enumerate(iterable), key=itemgetter(1))[0]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5369) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5370) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5371) def _extract_monotonic(iterator, indices):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5372)     'Non-decreasing indices, lazily consumed'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5373)     num_read = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5374)     for index in indices:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5375)         advance = index - num_read
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5376)         try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5377)             value = next(islice(iterator, advance, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5378)         except ValueError:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5379)             if advance != -1 or index < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5380)                 raise ValueError(f'Invalid index: {index}') from None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5381)         except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5382)             raise IndexError(index) from None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5383)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5384)             num_read += advance + 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5385)         yield value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5386) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5387) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5388) def _extract_buffered(iterator, index_and_position):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5389)     'Arbitrary index order, greedily consumed'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5390)     buffer = {}
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5391)     iterator_position = -1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5392)     next_to_emit = 0
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5393) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5394)     for index, order in index_and_position:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5395)         advance = index - iterator_position
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5396)         if advance:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5397)             try:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5398)                 value = next(islice(iterator, advance - 1, None))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5399)             except StopIteration:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5400)                 raise IndexError(index) from None
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5401)             iterator_position = index
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5402) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5403)         buffer[order] = value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5404) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5405)         while next_to_emit in buffer:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5406)             yield buffer.pop(next_to_emit)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5407)             next_to_emit += 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5408) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5409) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5410) def extract(iterable, indices, *, monotonic=False):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5411)     """Yield values at the specified indices.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5412) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5413)     Example:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5414) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5415)         >>> data = 'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz'
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5416)         >>> list(extract(data, [7, 4, 11, 11, 14]))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5417)         ['h', 'e', 'l', 'l', 'o']
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5418) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5419)     The *iterable* is consumed lazily and can be infinite.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5420) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5421)     When *monotonic* is false, the *indices* are consumed immediately
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5422)     and must be finite. When *monotonic* is true, *indices* are consumed
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5423)     lazily and can be infinite but must be non-decreasing.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5424) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5425)     Raises ``IndexError`` if an index lies beyond the iterable.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5426)     Raises ``ValueError`` for a negative index or for a decreasing
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5427)     index when *monotonic* is true.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5428)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5429) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5430)     iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5431)     indices = iter(indices)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5432) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5433)     if monotonic:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5434)         return _extract_monotonic(iterator, indices)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5435) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5436)     index_and_position = sorted(zip(indices, count()))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5437)     if index_and_position and index_and_position[0][0] < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5438)         raise ValueError('Indices must be non-negative')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5439)     return _extract_buffered(iterator, index_and_position)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5440) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5441) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5442) class serialize:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5443)     """Wrap a non-concurrent iterator with a lock to enforce sequential access.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5444) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5445)     Applies a non-reentrant lock around calls to ``__next__``, allowing
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5446)     iterator and generator instances to be shared by multiple consumer
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5447)     threads.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5448)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5449) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5450)     __slots__ = ('_iterator', '_lock')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5451) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5452)     def __init__(self, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5453)         self._iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5454)         self._lock = Lock()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5455) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5456)     def __iter__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5457)         return self
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5458) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5459)     def __next__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5460)         with self._lock:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5461)             return next(self._iterator)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5462) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5463)     def send(self, value, /):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5464)         """Send a value to a generator.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5465) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5466)         Raises AttributeError if not a generator.

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5488) def synchronized(func):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5489)     """Wrap an iterator-returning callable to make its iterators thread-safe.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5490) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5491)     Existing itertools and more-itertools can be wrapped so that their
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5492)     iterator instances are serialized.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5493) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5494)     For example, ``itertools.count`` does not make thread-safe instances,
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5495)     but that is easily fixed with::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5496) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5497)         atomic_counter = synchronized(itertools.count)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5498) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5499)     Can also be used as a decorator for generator functions definitions
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5500)     so that the generator instances are serialized::
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5501) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5502)         @synchronized
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5503)         def enumerate_and_timestamp(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5504)             for count, value in enumerate(iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5505)                 yield count, time_ns(), value
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5506) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5507)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5508) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5509)     @wraps(func)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5510)     def inner(*args, **kwargs):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5511)         iterator = func(*args, **kwargs)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5512)         return serialize(iterator)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5513) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5514)     return inner
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5515) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5516) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5517) def concurrent_tee(iterable, n=2):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5518)     """Variant of itertools.tee() but with guaranteed threading semantics.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5519) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5520)     Takes a non-threadsafe iterator as an input and creates concurrent
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5521)     tee objects for other threads to have reliable independent copies of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5522)     the data stream.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5523) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5524)     The new iterators are only thread-safe if consumed within a single thread.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5525)     To share just one of the new iterators across multiple threads, wrap it
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5526)     with :func:`serialize`.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5527)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5528) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5529)     if n < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5530)         raise ValueError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5531)     if n == 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5532)         return ()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5533)     iterator = _concurrent_tee(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5534)     result = [iterator]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5535)     for _ in range(n - 1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5536)         result.append(_concurrent_tee(iterator))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5537)     return tuple(result)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5538) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5539) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5540) class _concurrent_tee:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5541)     __slots__ = ('iterator', 'link', 'lock')
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5542) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5543)     def __init__(self, iterable):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5544)         if isinstance(iterable, _concurrent_tee):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5545)             self.iterator = iterable.iterator
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5546)             self.link = iterable.link
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5547)             self.lock = iterable.lock
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5548)         else:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5549)             self.iterator = iter(iterable)
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5550)             self.link = [None, None]
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5551)             self.lock = Lock()
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5552)

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5556)     def __next__(self):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5557)         link = self.link

^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5566) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5567) def subfactorial(n):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5568)     """Number of permutations of *n* elements with no fixed points.
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5569) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5570)     The :func:`subfactorial` function computes the length of
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5571)     :func:`derangements`.  For example, there are 1,854 ways to
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5572)     rearrange the letters in word "epsilon" without leaving any
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5573)     letter in its original position:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5574) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5575)         >>> from more_itertools import derangements, ilen
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5576)         >>> ilen(derangements('epsilon'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5577)         1854
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5578)         >>> subfactorial(len('epsilon'))
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5579)         1854
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5580) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5581)     Reference:  https://oeis.org/A000166
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5582) 
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5583)     """
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5584)     if n < 0:
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5585)         raise ValueError
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5586)     sf = adj = 1
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5587)     for i in range(n + 1):
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5588)         sf = sf * i + adj
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5589)         adj = -adj
^f46f9be (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 5590)     return sf
